Mrs. Whitefoot was sitting on the edge of it, and her bright eyes snapped with excitement and pleasure.
"I've found it!" she cried. "I've found it! It is just what I have been looking for."
"Found what?" Whitefoot asked. "I don't see anything but an old nest of Melody's."
"I've found the home we've been looking for, stupid," retorted Mrs. Whitefoot.
Still Whitefoot stared. "I don't see any house," said he.
Mrs. Whitefoot stamped her feet impatiently. "Right here, stupid," said she. "This old nest will make us the finest and safest home that ever was. No one will ever think of looking for us here. We must get busy at once and fix it up."
Even then Whitefoot didn't understand. Always he had lived either in a hole in the ground, or in a hollow stump or tree. How they were to live in that old nest he couldn't see at all.

===Chinese===
## XXVIII Mrs. Whitefoot Decides on a Home / XXVIII 白足太太决定选一个家

> 当白足太太拿定主意，
> 白足先生最好乖乖听话。
> ——林鼠白足

林鼠白足为自己的家感到非常自豪。他领着白足太太去的时候就展示给她看了。他确信她会立刻说这就是他们该住的地方。你还记得它在一个高高的枯树桩顶上，曾经是飞鼠提米的家。
"亲爱的，你觉得怎么样？"当他们到达小圆门口的时候，白足自豪地问道。
白足太太什么也没说，立刻走了进去。她去了好久好久，白足在外面焦急地等着。你知道，白足太太是一个非常仔细的小家伙，她从上到下检查了这座房子的里里外外。最后她出现在门口。
"你不觉得这是一座很棒的房子吗？"白足有点怯怯地问。
"就这类房子来说，它确实不错，"白足太太回答。
白足的心沉了下去。他不喜欢白足太太说话的那种语气。
"你到底是什么意思，亲爱的？"白足问。
"我是说，"白足太太以非常果断的方式回答，"这是一座非常好的冬天的房子，但夏天根本不合适。也就是说，它不适合我。首先，它太高了，如果我们有了宝宝，我会一直担心他们会掉下来。而且，我不喜欢夏天住在房子里。我觉得，白足，我们必须到处找找，找一个新的家。"
白足太太说着就开始往下爬。白足跟着。
"好吧，亲爱的，好吧，"他顺从地说。"你最清楚。我觉得这是一个非常好的家，但当然，如果你不喜欢，我们就去找另一个。"
白足太太什么也没说，领头下了树，白足乖乖地跟在后面。然后他们开始了耐心的搜索。白足太太似乎很清楚自己要什么，白足觉得好几个地方都能做很好的家，她都瞧不上。白足找到的一根好的空心木头她几乎看都没看。一个旧树桩根下有一个好洞，她只是把鼻子探进去看了看。白足跑来跑去、爬上爬下，已经开始累了。
他停下来休息，和白足太太走散了。过了一会儿，他听到她兴奋地叫他。他找到她的时候，她正坐在离地几英尺高的一棵小树上，坐在一个旧鸟巢的边缘。那是啄木鸟旋律曾经住过的巢。白足太太坐在巢的边缘，明亮的眼睛里闪着兴奋和快乐的光芒。
"我找到了！"她叫道。"我找到了！这正是我在找的东西。"
"找到什么？"白足问。"我只看到旋律的一个旧巢。"
"我找到了我们在找的家，笨蛋，"白足太太反驳道。
白足还是瞪着眼睛。"我没看到什么房子，"他说。
白足太太不耐烦地跺了跺脚。"就在这里，笨蛋，"她说。"这个旧巢会给我们一个有史以来最好最安全的家。没有人会想到在这里找我们。我们必须马上动手把它整理好。"
即使到了这时，白足还是不明白。他一直住在地洞里，或者空心树桩或空心树干里。他完全看不出他们怎么能住在这个旧鸟巢里。

===Original===
## XXIX Making Over an Old House / XXIX 改造一座旧房子

A home is always what you make it.
With love there you will ne'er forsake it.
- Whitefoot

Whitefoot climbed up to the old nest of Melody the Wood Thrush over the edge of which little Mrs. Whitefoot was looking down at him. It took Whitefoot hardly a moment to get up there, for the nest was only a few feet above the ground in a young tree, and you know Whitefoot is a very good climber.
He found Mrs. Whitefoot very much excited. She was delighted with that old nest and she showed it. For his part, Whitefoot couldn't see anything but a deserted old house of no use to anyone. To be sure, it had been a very good home in its time. It had been made of tiny twigs, stalks of old weeds, leaves, little fine roots and mud. It was still quite solid, and was firmly fixed in a crotch of the young tree. But Whitefoot couldn't see how it could be turned into a home for a Mouse. He said as much.
Little Mrs. Whitefoot became more excited than ever. "You dear old stupid," said she, "whatever is the matter with you? Don't you see that all we need do is to put a roof on, make an entrance on the under side, and make a soft comfortable bed inside to make it a delightful home?"
"I don't see why we don't make a new home altogether," protested Whitefoot. "It seems to me that hollow stub of mine is ever so much better than this. That has good solid walls, and we won't have to do a thing to it."
"I told you once before that it doesn't suit me for summer," replied little Mrs. Whitefoot rather sharply, because she was beginning to lose patience. "It will be all right for winter, but winter is a long way off. It may suit you for summer, but it doesn't suit me, and this place does. So this is where we are going to live."
"Certainly, my dear. Certainly," replied Whitefoot very meekly. "If you want to live here, here we will live. But I must confess it isn't clear to me yet how we are going to make a decent home out of this old nest."
"Don't you worry about that," replied Mrs. Whitefoot. "You can get the material, and I'll attend to the rest. Let us waste no time about it. I am anxious to get our home finished and to feel a little bit settled. I have already planned just what has got to be done and how we will do it. Now you go look for some nice soft, dry weed stalks and strips of soft bark, and moss and any other soft, tough material that you can find. Just get busy and don't stop to talk."
Of course Whitefoot did as he was told. He ran down to the ground and began to hunt for the things Mrs. Whitefoot wanted. He was very particular about it. He still didn't think much of her idea of making over that old home of Melody's, but if she would do it, he meant that she should have the very best of materials to do it with.
So back and forth from the ground to the old nest in the tree Whitefoot hurried, and presently there was quite a pile of weed stalks and soft grass and strips of bark in the old nest. Mrs. Whitefoot joined Whitefoot in hunting for just the right things, but she spent more time in arranging the material. Over that old nest she made a fine high roof. Down through the lower side she cut a little round doorway just big enough for them to pass through. Unless you happened to be underneath looking up, you never would have guessed there was an entrance at all. Inside was a snug, round room, and in this she made the softest and most comfortable of beds. As it began to look more and more like a home, Whitefoot himself became as excited and eager as Mrs. Whitefoot had been from the beginning. "It certainly is going to be a fine home," said Whitefoot.
"Didn't I tell you it would be?" retorted Mrs. Whitefoot.

===Chinese===
## XXIX Making Over an Old House / XXIX 改造一座旧房子

> 家永远是你建造的样子。
> 有爱在那里，你永远不会抛弃它。
> ——林鼠白足

白足爬上了啄木鸟旋律的旧巢，小巧的白足太太正从巢的边缘俯视着他。白足爬上去几乎没花什么时间，因为鸟巢就在一棵小树上离地几英尺的地方，你知道白足是一个很好的爬树能手。
他发现白足太太非常兴奋。她对那个旧巢非常满意，而且展示给了他看。在白足看来，他只看到一座被遗弃的旧房子，谁也用不了。当然，它曾经是一个非常好的家。它是用细小的树枝、旧杂草的茎、树叶、细小的根须和泥土做的。它仍然很结实，牢牢地卡在小树的树杈里。但白足看不出怎么能把它改造成一个老鼠的家。他把这说了出来。
白足太太变得更加兴奋了。"你这个亲爱的老笨蛋，"她说，"你怎么回事？你没看出来我们只需要加个屋顶，在底下开个入口，在里面铺一张柔软舒适的床，就能变成一个可爱的家了吗？"
"我看不出我们为什么不干脆造一个新家，"白足抗议道。"我觉得我的那个空心树桩比这个好多了。它有结实的墙壁，什么都不用做。"
"我早就告诉过你它不适合夏天住，"白足太太有点尖锐地回答，因为她开始失去耐心了。"冬天住没问题，但冬天还很远。它也许适合你夏天住，但不适合我，而这个地方适合。所以这就是我们要住的地方。"
"当然，亲爱的。当然，"白足非常顺从地回答。"如果你想住在这里，我们就住在这里。但我必须承认，我还是不明白我们怎么能把这个旧巢改造成一个像样的家。"
"你不用担心那个，"白足太太回答。"你去弄材料，剩下的我来管。我们别浪费时间了。我想赶快把我们的家弄好，安顿下来。我已经计划好了要做什么和怎么做。现在你去找一些又软又干的草茎、柔软的树皮条、苔藓，还有你能找到的其他柔软又结实的东西。赶快动手，别停下来说话。"
白足当然照她说的做了。他跑到地上，开始找白足太太要的东西。他非常仔细。他仍然不太赞成她改造旋律旧家的主意，但如果她要做，他要确保她用最好的材料。
于是白足在地面和树上的旧巢之间来回跑，不一会儿旧巢里就堆了一堆草茎、柔软的草和树皮条。白足太太和白足一起找合适的东西，但她花更多时间在整理材料上。她在旧巢上面做了一个漂亮的高屋顶。在底下那一侧，她开了一个刚好够他们通过的小圆洞。除非你碰巧在下面往上看，否则你永远猜不到那里有个入口。里面是一个舒适的圆形房间，她在里面做了最柔软最舒适的床。当它越来越像一个家的时候，白足自己也变得和白足太太一开始一样兴奋和急切。"这一定会是一个非常好的家，"白足说。
"我不是告诉过你会是这样的吗？"白足太太反驳道。

===Original===
## XXX The Whitefoots Enjoy Their New Home / XXX 白足一家享受他们的新家

No home is ever mean or poor
Where love awaits you at the door.
- Whitefoot

"There," said Mrs. Whitefoot, as she worked a strip of white birch bark into the roof of the new home she and Whitefoot had been building out of the old home of Melody the Wood Thrush, "this finishes the roof. I don't think any water will get through it even in the hardest rain."
"It is wonderful," declared Whitefoot admiringly. "Wherever did you learn to build such a house as this?"
"From my mother," replied Mrs. Whitefoot. "I was born in just such a home. It makes the finest kind of a home for Wood Mouse babies."
"You don't think there is danger that the wind will blow it down, do you?" ventured Whitefoot.
"Of course I don't," retorted little Mrs. Whitefoot scornfully. "Hasn't this old nest remained right where it is for over a year? Do you suppose that if I had thought there was the least bit of danger that it would blow down, I would have used it? Do credit me with a little sense, my dear."
"Yes'm, I do," replied Whitefoot meekly. "You are the most sensible person in all the Great World. I wasn't finding fault. You see, I have always lived in a hole in the ground or a hollow stump, or a hole in a tree, and I have not yet become used to a home that moves about and rocks as this one does when the wind blows. But if you say it is all right, why of course it is all right. Probably I will get used to it after awhile."
Whitefoot did get used to it. After living in it for a few days, it no longer seemed strange, and he no longer minded its swaying when the wind blew. The fact is, he rather enjoyed it. So Whitefoot and Mrs. Whitefoot settled down to enjoy their new home. Now and then they added a bit to it here and there.
Somehow Whitefoot felt unusually safe, safer than he had ever felt in any of his other homes. You see, he had seen several feathered folk alight close to it and not give it a second look. He knew that they had seen that home, but had mistaken it for what it had once been, the deserted home of one of their own number.
Whitefoot had chuckled. He had chuckled long and heartily. "If they make that mistake," said he to himself, "everybody else is likely to make it. That home of ours is right in plain sight, yet I do believe it is safer than the best hidden home I ever had before. Shadow the Weasel never will think of climbing up this little tree to look at an old nest, and Shadow is the one I am most afraid of."
It was only a day or two later that Buster Bear happened along that way. Now Buster is very fond of tender Wood Mouse. More than once Whitefoot had had a narrow escape from Buster's big claws as they tore open an old stump or dug into the ground after him. He saw Buster glance up at the new home without the slightest interest in those shrewd little eyes of his. Then Buster shuffled on to roll over an old log and lick up the ants he found under it. Again Whitefoot chuckled. "Yes, sir," said he. "It is the safest home I've ever had."
So Whitefoot and little Mrs. Whitefoot were very happy in the home which they had built, and for once in his life Whitefoot did very little worrying. Life seemed more beautiful than it had ever been before. And he almost forgot that there was such a thing as a hungry enemy.

===Chinese===
## XXX The Whitefoots Enjoy Their New Home / XXX 白足一家享受他们的新家

> 没有家是卑鄙或贫穷的，
> 如果有爱在门口等候。
> ——林鼠白足

"好了，"白足太太一边把一条白桦树皮编进她和白足一起用啄木鸟旋律的旧巢建造的新家的屋顶里，一边说，"屋顶完成了。我觉得即使下最大的雨，水也渗不进来。"
"太棒了，"白足赞赏地说。"你是在哪里学会造这样的房子的？"
"从我妈妈那里学的，"白足太太回答。"我就出生在这样一个家里。这是给林鼠宝宝最好的家。"
"你不担心风会把它吹倒吧？"白足试探着问。
"当然不担心，"小巧的白足太太轻蔑地反驳。"这个旧巢不是已经在原来的地方待了一年多了吗？你真的以为如果我觉得它有一点点被吹倒的危险，我还会用它吗？你对我有点信心好不好，亲爱的。"
"是的，夫人，我有信心，"白足顺从地回答。"你是整个大世界里最明智的人。我不是在挑毛病。你知道，我一直住在地洞里、空心树桩里或者树洞里，我还不习惯一个在风中会摇晃移动的家。但如果你说没问题，那当然没问题了。也许过一阵子我就会习惯的。"
白足确实习惯了。住了几天以后，它看起来不再陌生了，他也不再介意风把它吹得摇晃了。事实上，他反而有点喜欢这样了。于是白足和白足太太安顿下来享受他们的新家。他们时不时地在这里那里添上一些东西。
白足不知怎的感到特别安全，比他住过的任何其他家都安全。你知道，他看到好几只长翅膀的家伙落在附近，看都没多看一眼。他知道他们看到了那个家，但把它当成了它曾经的样子——他们同类中的某一个被遗弃的家。
白足咯咯地笑了。他笑得又长又开心。"如果他们搞错了，"他自言自语，"其他人很可能也会搞错。我们的家就在明处，但我真的相信它比我以前隐藏得最好的家还要安全。黄鼠狼影子绝不会想到爬上这棵小树来看一个旧巢，而影子是我最害怕的家伙。"
就一两天以后，巴斯特熊碰巧路过那里。巴斯特熊非常喜欢嫩嫩的林鼠肉。白足不止一次死里逃生，巴斯特的大爪子撕开老树桩或者在地上追他的时候。他看到巴斯特抬头看了一眼新家，精明的小眼睛里没有丝毫兴趣。然后巴斯特慢悠悠地走过去，翻倒一根旧木头，舔他发现的蚂蚁。白足又咯咯笑了。"是的，先生，"他说。"这是我住过的最安全的家。"
就这样，白足和小巧的白足太太在他们建造的家里非常快乐，白足有生以来第一次几乎不怎么担心了。生活似乎比以前任何时候都要美好。他几乎忘了世上还有饥饿的敌人这种东西。

===Original===
## XXXI Whitefoot Is Hurt / XXXI 林鼠白足受伤了

The hurts that hardest are to bear
Come from those for whom we care.
- Whitefoot

Whitefoot was hurt. Yes, sir, Whitefoot was hurt. He was very much hurt. It wasn't a bodily hurt; it was an inside hurt. It was a hurt that made his heart ache. And to make it worse, he couldn't understand it at all. One evening he had been met at the little round doorway by little Mrs. Whitefoot.
"You can't come in," said she.
"Why can't I?" demanded Whitefoot, in the greatest surprise.
"Never mind why. You can't, and that is all there is to it," replied Mrs. Whitefoot.
"You mean I can't ever come in any more?" asked Whitefoot.
"I don't know about that," replied Mrs. Whitefoot, "but you can't come in now, nor for some time. I think the best thing you can do is to go back to your old home in the hollow stub."
Whitefoot stared at little Mrs. Whitefoot quite as if he thought she had gone crazy. Then he lost his temper. "I guess I'll come in if I want to," said he. "This home is quite as much my home as it is yours. You have no right to keep me out of it. Just you get out of my way."
But little Mrs. Whitefoot didn't get out of his way, and do what he would, Whitefoot couldn't get in. You see she quite filled that little round doorway. Finally, he had to give up trying. Three times he came back and each time he found little Mrs. Whitefoot in the doorway. And each time she drove him away. Finally, for lack of any other place to go to, he returned to his old home in the old stub. Once he had thought this the finest home possible, but now somehow it didn't suit him at all. The truth is he missed little Mrs. Whitefoot, and so what had once been a home was now only a place in which to hide and sleep.
Whitefoot's anger did not last long. It was replaced by that hurt feeling. He felt that he must have done something little Mrs. Whitefoot did not like, but though he thought and thought he couldn't remember a single thing. Several times he went back to see if Mrs. Whitefoot felt any differently, but found she didn't. Finally she told him rather sharply to go away and stay away. After that Whitefoot didn't venture over to the new home. He would sometimes sit a short distance away and gaze at it longingly. All the joy had gone out of the beautiful springtime for him. He was quite as unhappy as he had been before he met little Mrs. Whitefoot. You see, he was even more lonely than he had been then. And added to this loneliness was that hurt feeling, which made it ever and ever so much worse. It was very hard to bear.
"If I could understand it, it wouldn't be so bad," he kept saying over and over again to himself, "but I don't understand it. I don't understand why Mrs. Whitefoot doesn't love me any more."

===Chinese===
## XXXI Whitefoot Is Hurt / XXXI 林鼠白足受伤了

> 最难承受的伤害
> 来自我们在乎的人。
> ——林鼠白足

白足受伤了。是的，先生，白足受伤了。他伤得很重。那不是身体上的伤；是内心的伤。是一种让他的心痛的伤。更糟糕的是，他完全不明白这是怎么回事。一天晚上，他在小圆门口被白足太太拦住了。
"你不能进来，"她说。
"为什么不能？"白足非常惊讶地问。
"别管为什么。你就是不能，就是这样，"白足太太回答。
"你的意思是我再也不能进来了？"白足问。
"那倒不一定，"白足太太回答，"但你现在不能进来，一段时间内也不行。我觉得你能做的最好的事就是回到你在空心树桩里的旧家去。"
白足瞪着白足太太，好像觉得她疯了。然后他发了脾气。"我想进来就会进来的，"他说。"这个家是我的家，和你的一样。你没有权利把我关在外面。你给我让开。"
但白足太太没有让开，无论他怎么做，白足就是进不去。你知道她完全堵住了那个小圆门。最后他不得不放弃了。他三次回来，每次都发现白足太太堵在门口。每次都把他赶走。最后因为没有别的地方可去，他回到了他在旧树桩里的旧家。他曾以为这是最好的家，但现在不知怎的它一点也不适合他了。事实上他想念白足太太，所以曾经是家的地方现在只是一个藏身和睡觉的地方。
白足的愤怒没有持续多久。取而代之的是那种受伤的感觉。他觉得他一定是做了什么白足太太不喜欢的事，但无论怎么想他都想不起来。好几次他回去看看白足太太是不是改变了想法，但发现她没有。最后她相当严厉地叫他走开别再来了。从那以后白足不敢再去新家了。他有时会坐在不远处，渴望地凝望着它。美丽的春天对他来说已经没有了快乐。他和遇到白足太太之前一样不快乐。你要知道，他甚至比那时更孤独了。再加上这种受伤的感觉，让一切变得更加糟糕。实在难以承受。
"如果我能弄明白就不会这么难受了，"他一遍又一遍地对自己说，"但我弄不明白。我不明白白足太太为什么不再爱我了。"

===Original===
## XXXII The Surprise / XXXII 惊喜

Surprises sometimes are so great
You're tempted to believe in fate.
- Whitefoot

One never-to-be forgotten evening Whitefoot met Mrs. Whitefoot and she invited him to come back to their home. Of course Whitefoot was delighted.
"Sh-h-h," said little Mrs. Whitefoot, as Whitefoot entered the snug little room of the house they had built in the old nest of Melody the Wood Thrush. Whitefoot hesitated. In the first place, it was dark in there. In the second place, he had the feeling that somehow that little bedroom seemed crowded. It hadn't been that way the last time he was there. Mrs. Whitefoot was right in front of him, and she seemed very much excited about something.
Presently she crowded to one side. "Come here and look," said she.
Whitefoot looked. In the middle of a soft bed of moss was a squirming mass of legs and funny little heads. At first that was all Whitefoot could make out.
"Don't you think this is the most wonderful surprise that ever was?" whispered little Mrs. Whitefoot. "Aren't they darlings? Aren't you proud of them?"
By this time Whitefoot had made out that that squirming mass of legs and heads was composed of baby Mice. He counted them. There were four. "Whose are they, and what are they doing here?" Whitefoot asked in a queer voice.
"Why, you old stupid, they are yours-yours and mine," declared little Mrs. Whitefoot. "Did you ever, ever see such beautiful babies? Now I guess you understand why I kept you away from here."
Whitefoot shook his head. "No," said he, "I don't understand at all. I don't see yet what you drove me away for."
"Why, you blessed old dear, there wasn't room for you when those babies came; I had to have all the room there was. It wouldn't have done to have had you running in and out and disturbing them when they were so tiny. I had to be alone with them, and that is why I made you go off and live by yourself. I am so proud of them, I don't know what to do. Aren't you proud, Whitefoot? Aren't you the proudest Wood Mouse in all the Green Forest?"
Of course Whitefoot should have promptly said that he was, but the truth is, Whitefoot wasn't proud at all. You see, he was so surprised that he hadn't yet had time to feel that they were really his. In fact, just then he felt a wee bit jealous of them. It came over him that they would take all the time and attention of little Mrs. Whitefoot. So Whitefoot didn't answer that question. He simply sat and stared at those four squirming babies.
Finally little Mrs. Whitefoot gently pushed him out and followed him. "Of course," said she, "there isn't room for you to stay here now. You will have to sleep in your old home because there isn't room in here for both of us and the babies too."
Whitefoot's heart sank. He had thought that he was to stay and that everything would be just as it had been before. "Can't I come over here any more?" he asked rather timidly.
"What a foolish question!" cried little Mrs. Whitefoot. "Of course you can. You will have to help take care of these babies. Just as soon as they are big enough, you will have to help teach them how to hunt for food and how to watch out for danger, and all the things that a wise Wood Mouse knows. Why, they couldn't get along without you. Neither could I," she added softly.
At that Whitefoot felt better. And suddenly there was a queer swelling in his heart. It was the beginning of pride, pride in those wonderful babies.
"You have given me the best surprise that ever was, my dear," said Whitefoot softly. "Now I think I will go and look for some supper."
So now we will leave Whitefoot and his family. You see there are two very lively little people of the Green Forest who demand attention and insist on having it. They are Buster Bear's Twins, and this is to be the title of the next book.

===Chinese===
## XXXII The Surprise / XXXII 惊喜

> 有时候惊喜如此之大，
> 让你忍不住相信命运。
> ——林鼠白足

一个永生难忘的夜晚，白足遇到了白足太太，她邀请他回到他们的家。白足当然很高兴。
"嘘——"当白足走进他们在啄木鸟旋律旧巢里建造的房子的温暖小房间时，白足太太轻声说。白足犹豫了。首先，里面很暗。其次，他有一种感觉，不知怎的那个小卧室好像很拥挤。他上次来的时候不是这样的。白足太太就站在他面前，她似乎对什么事情非常兴奋。
过了一会儿，她挪到了一边。"过来看看，"她说。
白足看了看。在一张柔软的苔藓床中间，有一团蠕动着的小腿和搞笑的小脑袋。一开始白足只看到这些。
"你不觉得这是有史以来最了不起的惊喜吗？"白足太太小声说。"他们是不是很可爱？你是不是为他们感到骄傲？"
到了这时白足才看出来那团蠕动着的小腿和小脑袋是小老鼠宝宝。他数了数。有四只。"他们是谁的？他们在这里做什么？"白足用一种奇怪的声音问。
"哎呀，你这个老笨蛋，他们是你的——你和我的，"白足太太宣布。"你见过这么漂亮的宝宝吗？现在你大概明白为什么我把你赶出去了吧。"
白足摇了摇头。"不，"他说，"我一点也不明白。我还是不明白你为什么要赶我走。"
"哎呀，你这个亲爱的老宝贝，那些宝宝来的时候没地方给你待了；我需要所有的空间。他们在那么小的时候，不能让你跑进跑出打扰他们。我必须和他们单独在一起，这就是为什么我让你自己去住。我太为他们骄傲了，简直不知道该怎么办。你不骄傲吗，白足？你不是整个绿森林里最骄傲的林鼠吗？"
白足本应该立刻说他是的，但事实上白足一点也不骄傲。你要知道，他太惊讶了，还没有来得及感受到他们真的是他的。事实上，那一刻他还有点嫉妒他们。他想到他们会占用白足太太所有的时间和注意力。所以白足没有回答那个问题。他就那么坐着，盯着那四个蠕动着的小宝宝。
最后白足太太轻轻地把他推了出去，自己也跟了出来。"当然，"她说，"现在没有地方让你待在这里了。你得睡在你自己的旧家里，因为这里没有地方容纳我们俩加上宝宝们了。"
白足的心沉了下去。他原以为他会留下来，一切都会和以前一样。"我不能到这边来了吗？"他有点怯怯地问。
"多傻的问题啊！"白足太太叫道。"你当然可以。你还得帮忙照顾这些宝宝呢。等他们长大一点，你就要帮忙教他们怎么找吃的，怎么提防危险，以及一只聪明的林鼠知道的所有事情。他们不能没有你。我也不能，"她柔声补充道。
听到这些，白足觉得好受多了。突然一种奇怪的膨胀感在他心里升起。那是骄傲的开始，对那些了不起的宝宝的骄傲。
"你给了我有史以来最好的惊喜，亲爱的，"白足温柔地说。"现在我想去找点晚餐了。"
现在我们就要离开白足和他的家人了。你知道绿森林里有两个非常活泼的小家伙，他们需要关注，而且坚持要得到关注。他们是巴斯特熊的双胞胎，这就是下一本书的书名。
